### 337. assistant (2026-07-04T03:47:41.380Z)

## Summary

Under `cloud-serving`, concurrent `/api/process` requests are processed **one video at a time**. The in-process job drain is a `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1)` (`backend/api/job_worker.py:49`), and a process job **holds that single worker while it bucket-polls its L4 Cloud Run Job execution to completion**. So a second video's job stays `queued` until the first finishes — even though Cloud Run Jobs can run many executions in parallel. Throughput is capped at 1 concurrent video regardless of L4 quota.

## Runtime evidence (deployed alpha, 2026-07-04)

While the main gs:// CUJ job `d16654d1…` was `running`/`dispatching (cloud_run)` and bucket-polling its L4 execution `rally-worker-k49kg` (~13 min), a second `/api/process` job `3d44c5eb…` stayed `status=queued`, `started_at=null` the **entire** time — it only became eligible after the first job completed. Compile jobs queue behind long process polls the same way.

## Code

- `backend/api/job_worker.py:49` — `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1, thread_name_prefix="jobworker")`.
- The rationale (job_worker.py:28-31) is **truly-local-specific**: *"a single self-hosted box serializes GPU work, and the Gemini provider is rate-fragile under parallel uploads."*
- `backend/orchestration.py` `_maybe_dispatch_remote` → `compute.run_infer(spec)` **blocks** on the bucket-poll (`backend/compute/cloud_run.py`, `poll_until`) inside the single drain worker.
- The O2 risk (one stuck job blocks all) is already noted at job_worker.py:33-41.

## Why it's wrong for cloud-serving specifically

Neither rationale applies under `deployment.compute_target=cloud_run`:
- **GPU work is NOT on the box** — it's on ephemeral L4 Job executions. The control-plane box only *dispatches + polls* (I/O-bound). Serializing it throttles nothing local.
- **No Gemini** — the alpha is WASB-only (`skip_ai_handoff=true`), so the rate-fragility concern is moot; and even with Gemini ON later, the handoff runs inside the L4 job, not the control-plane box.

So the single-worker serialization is an unnecessary bottleneck for the cloud-serving profile: 5 videos take 5× the wall-time of 1, when they could overlap.

## Proposed fix

Two options (not mutually exclusive):

1. **Non-blocking remote poll via the existing WAIT_AND_RESUME machinery (preferred).** The park/resume hook already exists (`JobWaiting` → `set_job_waiting(next_poll_at)` → release the thread → re-claim via `claim_due_job`, driven by `_arm_wake_timer`; job_worker.py:55-65). Make the Cloud Run bucket-poll **park between marker checks** instead of blocking: dispatch the L4 execution, persist the done-marker/execution URI, raise `JobWaiting(delay)`, and on re-claim do one quick marker check. Then a **single** worker juggles many in-flight L4 executions (each parked between fast checks) — parallelism without adding threads, and the single-box/Gemini concerns stay satisfied.
2. **Profile-aware drain concurrency.** Allow `max_workers > 1` when `compute_target == cloud_run` (dispatch/poll is I/O-bound), bounded by a config knob (e.g. `serving.max_concurrent_remote_jobs`) to cap concurrent L4 executions (and thus spend / job quota). Keep `max_workers=1` for truly-local (unchanged).

Bound concurrency either way so a burst can't fan out unbounded paid L4 executions. Compile (CPU, in-process) should stay bounded to the control-plane instance's resources — consider a separate small pool or a lane so a long process poll can't starve compiles (related to the "compile serializes behind process jobs" note in the A1 runlog).

Also still open (job_worker.py:40): a per-job wall-clock timeout so one hung job can't wedge a lane.

## Related

Surfaced during the A1 alpha serving deploy (see #461, #463, runlog PR #464). This is the throughput/scale gap; the others are correctness/feature gaps.

1	"""Google Cloud Storage backend — first-class object buckets.
2	
3	Uses ``google-cloud-storage``, imported lazily so ``import backend.storage`` never pulls it;
4	install with the ``storage-gcs`` extra. Credentials come from Application Default Credentials
5	(``GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`` pointing at a service-account JSON, or workload identity) —
6	never from config. A ``client`` may be injected for tests.
7	"""
8	
9	from __future__ import annotations
10	
11	from datetime import timedelta
12	from importlib import import_module
13	from typing import Any, Iterator, Optional
14	
15	from backend.storage.base import StorageBackend
16	from backend.storage.ref import StorageRef, StorageStat
17	
18	
19	class GCSStorage(StorageBackend):
20	    def __init__(
21	        self,
22	        *,
23	        client: Any = None,
24	        project: Optional[str] = None,
25	        signed_url_ttl: int = 3600,
26	        **_ignored,
27	    ) -> None:
28	        self._ttl = signed_url_ttl
29	        if client is not None:
30	            self._client = client
31	            return
32	        storage_module: Any = import_module("google.cloud.storage")
33	        self._client = storage_module.Client(project=project)
34	
35	    def _blob(self, ref: StorageRef):
36	        return self._client.bucket(ref.bucket).blob(ref.key)
37	
38	    def fetch_to_local(self, ref, dst=None, *, overwrite=False) -> str:
39	        if dst is None:
40	            raise ValueError("GCSStorage.fetch_to_local requires a dst path")
41	        import os
42	
43	        dst = str(dst)
44	        if os.path.exists(dst) and not overwrite:
45	            return dst
46	        os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(dst) or ".", exist_ok=True)
47	        self._blob(ref).download_to_filename(dst)
48	        return dst
49	
50	    def put(self, local_path, ref, *, content_type=None) -> StorageRef:
51	        self._blob(ref).upload_from_filename(str(local_path), content_type=content_type)
52	        return ref
53	
54	    def exists(self, ref) -> bool:
55	        return bool(self._blob(ref).exists())
56	
57	    def stat(self, ref) -> StorageStat:
58	        b = self._blob(ref)
59	        b.reload()
60	        return StorageStat(
61	            size=int(b.size or 0),
62	            modified=b.updated,
63	            etag=b.etag,
64	            content_type=b.content_type,
65	        )
66	
67	    def list(self, prefix) -> Iterator[StorageRef]:
68	        for blob in self._client.list_blobs(prefix.bucket, prefix=prefix.key):
69	            yield StorageRef(
70	                "gcs", prefix.bucket, blob.name, f"gcs://{prefix.bucket}/{blob.name}"
71	            )
72	
73	    def delete(self, ref) -> None:
74	        self._blob(ref).delete()
75	
76	    def signed_url(self, ref, *, ttl=3600, method="GET") -> str:
77	        return self._blob(ref).generate_signed_url(
78	            version="v4", expiration=timedelta(seconds=ttl or self._ttl), method=method
79	        )

357	    def claim_due_job(self) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
358	        """Atomically claim ONE runnable job — 'queued', or 'waiting' whose ``next_poll_at`` is due —
359	        flipping it to 'running'. Compare-and-set on (id, status) so concurrent workers never
360	        double-claim (the DB row-claim is the only coordination needed; no master). Earliest-due
361	        first. Returns the claimed job dict, or None if nothing is runnable."""
362	        try:
363	            with self._connect() as conn:
364	                cur = conn.cursor()
365	                row = cur.execute(
366	                    "SELECT id, status FROM jobs WHERE status='queued' "
367	                    "OR (status='waiting' AND (next_poll_at IS NULL OR next_poll_at <= CURRENT_TIMESTAMP)) "
368	                    "ORDER BY COALESCE(next_poll_at, created_at) ASC LIMIT 1"
369	                ).fetchone()
370	                if not row:
371	                    return None
372	                cur.execute(
373	                    "UPDATE jobs SET status='running', started_at=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP "
374	                    "WHERE id=? AND status=?",
375	                    (row["id"], row["status"]),
376	                )
377	                conn.commit()
378	                if cur.rowcount != 1:
379	                    return None  # lost the race to a concurrent worker
380	            return self.get_job(row["id"])
381	        except Exception as e:
382	            logger.error(f"Failed to claim a due job: {e}")
383	            return None
384	
385	    def fail_stale_jobs(self, reason: str = "interrupted by server restart") -> int:
386	        """Crash recovery: any job still queued/running from a previous process is dead
387	        (the executor is in-process). Mark failed so clients see a terminal state, not a
388	        hang. Returns the number of jobs swept.
389	
390	        NOTE: ``recover_stale_jobs`` is the preferred startup call — it RE-QUEUES stale
391	        ``compile`` jobs (idempotent CPU stitch, safe to retry) instead of failing them. This
392	        blanket-fail method stays for callers/tests that want the old behaviour."""
393	        try:
394	            with self._connect() as conn:
395	                cur = conn.cursor()
396	                cur.execute(
397	                    "UPDATE jobs SET status='failed', error=?, finished_at=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP "
398	                    "WHERE status IN ('queued', 'running')",
399	                    (reason,),
400	                )
401	                conn.commit()
402	                return cur.rowcount
403	        except Exception as e:
404	            logger.error(f"Failed to sweep stale jobs: {e}")
405	            return 0

def _maybe_dispatch_remote(
    cfg,
    db,
    req: "ProcessRequest",
    video_id: str,
    seg_name: str,
    val_results,
    play_wm: int,
    cand_wm: int,
    notify,
) -> Optional[Tuple[Dict[str, Any], bool]]:
    """Cloud-serving (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M6 → the API path): when ``deployment.compute_target
    == "cloud_run"``, run the heavy DETECT on a Cloud Run GPU Job and INGEST its rally intervals into
    THIS DB, so the rest of the flow (query → compile/local-stitch) is unchanged.

    Returns ``(response_dict, ran)`` when the cloud path is taken — ``ran`` is whether the cloud job
    actually EXECUTED (``False`` for a pre-dispatch config rejection, so the observability report
    doesn't claim a fake segmentation run) — or ``None`` to fall through to the in-process segmenter
    (**DEFAULT-OFF**: ``get_compute_backend`` returns ``None`` for every non-``cloud_run`` target, so
    the in-process path stays byte-identical)."""
    from backend.compute import ComputeJobSpec, get_compute_backend

    def _failed(msg: str, ran: bool) -> Tuple[Dict[str, Any], bool]:
        # Shape-match the in-process segmenter-fail branch + drop any partial NEW rows (id > play_wm);
        # prior good rows (id <= play_wm) are preserved (destroy-AFTER-confirm).
        db.prune_segments(video_id, play_after=play_wm, cand_after=cand_wm)
        # Provenance even on failure, and HONEST about what ran: `path` is "cloud_run" only when the
        # job actually dispatched + executed remotely (ran=True, e.g. a non-zero worker rc / ingest
        # error); a pre-dispatch reject (ran=False) ran NOWHERE → "not_run". `target` records the
        # intended backend, `requested` the picker choice, `dispatched` whether the remote job ran.
        return (
            {
                "video_id": video_id,
                "status": "failed",
                "message": msg,
                "results": [r.model_dump() for r in val_results],
                "play_segments": [],
                "compute": {
                    "path": "cloud_run" if ran else "not_run",
                    "requested": req.compute,
                    "target": "cloud_run",
                    "dispatched": ran,
                },
            },
            ran,
        )

    # SPA compute-picker (item 3): a per-request choice overrides the server's configured default.
    # "local" forces the in-process segmenter; "cloud" forces a Cloud Run dispatch (guarded by

1	"""Pluggable storage layer: local (default) + S3/GCS first-class buckets + Google Drive.
2	
3	URI-driven (see ``ref.parse_uri``). The package import is cheap — cloud SDKs are imported
4	lazily, only when a non-local bucket URI is actually used (install the ``storage-s3`` /
5	``storage-gcs`` extras). The default ``local`` backend needs no third-party deps and preserves
6	today's behaviour byte-for-byte; ``gdrive://`` likewise needs none — it reads a locally MOUNTED
7	Google Drive (Google Drive for Desktop) as plain files.
8	
9	Worker/dispatcher entry points speak URIs via the thin helpers ``fetch`` / ``put`` /
10	``list_uris`` / ``get_storage``; ``local://`` (and bare paths) make ``fetch`` an identity
11	passthrough. See ``docs/DECOUPLED_COMPUTE/``.
12	"""
13

15	
16	import os
17	import shutil

19	from typing import Any, Dict, Iterator, Optional
20	
21	from backend.storage.base import StorageBackend
22	from backend.storage.ref import StorageRef, StorageStat, parse_uri, resolve_input_uri

41	def _storage_dict(storage_config: Any) -> Dict[str, Any]:
42	    """Coerce a StorageConfig (Pydantic) / dict / None into a plain settings dict."""
43	    if storage_config is None:
44	        return {}
45	    if hasattr(storage_config, "model_dump"):
46	        return storage_config.model_dump()  # type: ignore[no-any-return]
47	    return dict(storage_config)

50	def resolve_input_ref(raw: str, storage_config: Any = None) -> StorageRef:
51	    """Resolve a user-supplied input (a path or storage URI) into a ``StorageRef`` under the input
52	    config (``input_backend``/``input_root``). Raises ``ValueError`` for an unsupported URI scheme —
53	    callers at the API/CLI boundary catch that and return a clean client error (never a 500).
54	
55	    Use ``ref.backend`` to branch local-vs-remote and ``ref.key`` for the resolved LOCAL path (e.g.
56	    ``local://…`` is stripped to the bare path), rather than stat-ing the raw URI string."""
57	    sc = _storage_dict(storage_config)
58	    uri = resolve_input_uri(
59	        raw,
60	        input_backend=sc.get("input_backend", "local"),
61	        input_root=sc.get("input_root", ""),
62	    )
63	    return parse_uri(uri)
64	
65	
66	def input_is_local(raw: str, storage_config: Any = None) -> bool:
67	    """True if ``raw`` resolves (under the input config) to a LOCAL filesystem path — i.e. a
68	    plain on-disk file the caller can stat/validate directly. False for a bucket / Drive URI
69	    that must be fetched first. Raises ``ValueError`` for an unsupported scheme (same as
70	    ``resolve_input_ref``)."""
71	    return resolve_input_ref(raw, storage_config).backend == "local"
72	
73	
74	def acquire_local_input(
75	    raw: str, storage_config: Any = None, *, scratch_parent: "str | None" = None
76	) -> str:
77	    """Resolve ``raw`` (a path or storage URI) to a LOCAL file path ready for processing.
78	
79	    Local / bare paths are returned UNCHANGED (identity passthrough, no copy — today's behaviour
80	    byte-for-byte). A bucket / Drive source (``gs://``/``s3://``/``gdrive://``, or a bare name under a
81	    non-local ``input_root``/``input_backend``) is downloaded into a fresh scratch dir and the local
82	    path returned. The caller owns successful remote scratch copies; use
83	    ``cleanup_acquired_local_input`` when the consumer has finished reading the returned path."""
84	    sc = _storage_dict(storage_config)
85	    ref = resolve_input_ref(raw, sc)
86	    if ref.backend == "local":
87	        return (
88	            ref.key
89	        )  # identity — no temp, no copy, byte-identical to passing the path directly
90	    scratch = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="rally-input-", dir=scratch_parent)
91	    try:
92	        return fetch(ref.uri, os.path.join(scratch, ref.name or "in.mp4"), config=sc)
93	    except Exception:
94	        shutil.rmtree(scratch, ignore_errors=True)
95	        raise
96

51	def parse_uri(uri: str) -> StorageRef:
52	    """Parse a storage URI (or a bare filesystem path) into a ``StorageRef``.
53	
54	    A string with no ``scheme://`` prefix (including Windows ``C:\\...`` paths, which
55	    have no ``//``) is treated as a local filesystem path — preserving today's behaviour.
56	    """
57	    s = str(uri)
58	    m = _SCHEME_RE.match(s)
59	    if not m:
60	        return StorageRef("local", "", s, f"local://{s}")
61	    scheme = m.group(1).lower()
62	    rest = s[m.end() :]
63	    if scheme == "gs":
64	        scheme = "gcs"
65	    if scheme in ("local", "gdrive", "gdrive-api"):
66	        # Path-keyed, no bucket: local FS, a path under the mounted Drive, or a path under a Drive-API
67	        # root folder (gdrive-api resolves the path → a Drive file id).
68	        return StorageRef(scheme, "", rest, s)
69	    if scheme in _KNOWN:
70	        bucket, _, key = rest.partition("/")
71	        return StorageRef(scheme, bucket, key, s)
72	    raise ValueError(f"unsupported storage URI scheme: {scheme}:// (in {s!r})")
73	
74	
75	def resolve_input_uri(
76	    raw: str, *, input_backend: str = "local", input_root: str = ""
77	) -> str:
78	    """Resolve a user-supplied input reference into a storage URI (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M2).
79	
80	    Precedence (first match wins):
81	      1. An explicit ``scheme://`` on ``raw`` (``gs://``, ``s3://``, ``local://``, ``gdrive://``) → as-is.
82	      2. An ABSOLUTE local path (``/data/x.mp4``, ``C:\\…``, ``C:/…``) → as-is (always local).
83	      3. A bare/relative name + a non-empty ``input_root`` → joined under it
84	         (``"gs://bucket/videos"`` + ``"x.mp4"`` → ``"gs://bucket/videos/x.mp4"``).
85	      4. A bare/relative name + ``input_backend != "local"`` → prefixed (``"gcs"`` + ``"bucket/x"`` →
86	         ``"gcs://bucket/x"``).
87	      5. Otherwise → ``raw`` unchanged (a relative/bare LOCAL path — today's behaviour).
88	
89	    So the default (``input_backend="local"``, ``input_root=""``) is an identity passthrough for every
90	    path used today, preserving byte-for-byte behaviour.
91	    """
92	    s = str(raw)
93	    if _SCHEME_RE.match(s):  # 1. explicit scheme wins
94	        return s
95	    # 2. an anchored LOCAL path is always local, never joined. Detect this OS-INDEPENDENTLY —
96	    #    os.path.isabs is platform- AND Python-version-dependent (on Windows + py3.13 a POSIX-style
97	    #    "/data/x" is NOT absolute). Anchored == a leading "/" or "\\", or a Windows drive prefix "X:".
98	    if s.startswith(("/", "\\")) or (len(s) >= 2 and s[1] == ":" and s[0].isalpha()):
99	        return s
100	    if input_root:  # 3. join a relative name under the configured root
101	        return input_root.rstrip("/") + "/" + s.lstrip("/")
102	    if (
103	        input_backend and input_backend != "local"
104	    ):  # 4. prefix a bucket/key with the declared backend
105	        return f"{input_backend}://{s}"
106	    return s  # 5. bare/relative local path — unchanged
107

277	def test_cloud_ingest_failure_marks_failed_no_leak(cloud_env):
278	    """A rc=0 marker but a MISSING intervals.csv (a partial push) must fail cleanly + leak no rows
279	    (destroy-AFTER-confirm), not 500 or hang."""
280	    db, _bucket, monkeypatch = cloud_env
281	
282	    class _NoCsvClient(_FakeCloudClient):
283	        def run_job(self, job_name, argv, *, region, project=None):
284	            self.calls.append(list(argv))
285	            out_uri = argv[argv.index("--out-uri") + 1].rstrip("/")
286	            done_uri = argv[argv.index("--done-uri") + 1]
287	            outputs = os.path.join(out_uri, "outputs", self.video_id)
288	            os.makedirs(outputs, exist_ok=True)
289	            with open(os.path.join(outputs, "report.json"), "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
290	                json.dump({"video_id": self.video_id, "status": "success"}, f)
291	            # deliberately DO NOT write intervals.csv
292	            os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(done_uri), exist_ok=True)
293	            with open(done_uri, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
294	                json.dump({"video_id": self.video_id, "returncode": 0}, f)
295	            return "exec"
296	
297	    _inject_fake(monkeypatch, _NoCsvClient())
298	    out = m._execute_processing(
299	        m.ProcessRequest(video_path="gs://b/clip.mp4", segmenter="wasb_hybrid")
300	    )
301	    assert out["status"] == "failed"
302	    assert "could not read the result" in out["message"]
303	    assert db.query_play_segments(out["video_id"], {}) == []

323	class _InProcSeg:
324	    """Minimal in-process segmenter (writes one play row) — proves the local path actually ran."""
325	
326	    def __init__(self, db_, cfg_):
327	        self.db = db_
328	
329	    def process_video(self, vid, path, *a, **k):
330	        sid = self.db.add_play_segment(vid, 0.0, 5.0)
331	        return [
332	            {
333	                "id": sid,
334	                "start_time": 0.0,
335	                "end_time": 5.0,
336	                "duration": 5.0,
337	                "metadata": {},
338	            }
339	        ]

483	    """End-to-end through the public API: POST /api/process (gs:// input) → the inline worker dispatches

512	    cloud_run server, POST /api/process with compute='local' must persist (jobs.compute) + reconstruct
513	    (_job_to_request) the choice and run IN-PROCESS — not dispatch to Cloud Run. (An earlier cut
514	    dropped compute in create_job/_job_to_request, silently nullifying the picker through /api/process.)"""

595	def test_cloud_dispatch_stamps_compute_provenance(cloud_env):
596	    """A cloud run stamps result['compute'] with path=cloud_run + the REAL Cloud Run execution name
597	    (the GCP-verifiable proof) + the requested choice + job/region/outputs — so the intended path is
598	    provable from the job result alone (no guessing from segment metadata)."""
599	    _db, _bucket, monkeypatch = cloud_env
600	    _inject_fake(monkeypatch, _FakeCloudClient())
601	
602	    out = m._execute_processing(
603	        m.ProcessRequest(
604	            video_path="gs://b/clip.mp4", segmenter="wasb_hybrid", compute="cloud"
605	        )
606	    )
607	    prov = out["compute"]
608	    assert prov["path"] == "cloud_run"
609	    assert prov["requested"] == "cloud"
610	    assert (
611	        prov["execution"] == "exec-1"
612	    )  # the fake client's execution name (real one in prod)
613	    assert prov["outputs_uri"].endswith("/outputs/vidCloud/")
614	    assert prov["job"] and prov["region"]  # the dispatch coordinates are recorded
615

class DeploymentConfig(BaseModel):
    """Deployment profile — one switch that selects a coherent bundle of compute + storage
    knobs (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M1, ``docs/COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING/README.md`` §4.3).

    **Pure / additive:** the empty default profile (``""``) applies NO preset, so the pipeline
    behaves exactly as before this section existed. A profile is opt-in (``config.json``
    ``deployment.profile`` or the ``DEPLOYMENT_PROFILE`` env var); auto-detect only ever
    *suggests* one (D15 — cloud spend is never entered silently). Nothing in the core reads
    these fields yet (M1): selecting a profile works purely by pre-setting the EXISTING knobs
    (``indexing.detector_impl`` / ``skip_ai_handoff``, ``storage.backend``) in the loader.

    Valid values mirror ``backend/config/presets.py`` (parity-pinned by a unit test)."""

    # "" = no profile (today's manual knobs). Others apply presets.PROFILE_PRESETS.
    profile: Literal["", "truly-local", "cloud-serving", "cpu-mock"] = ""
    # Where the core runs. Set by the profile preset; overridable in config.json. Inert in M1
    # (recorded for observability; consumed by the ComputeBackend seam from M2+).
    compute_target: Literal["", "local_gpu", "cloud_run", "cpu_mock"] = ""


class BillingConfig(BaseModel):
    """Per-job cost ledger (COMPUTE_DECOUPLED_SERVING M8, D8).

    **DEFAULT-OFF:** ``enabled=False`` → nothing records cost (the v6 cost columns stay NULL =
    today's behaviour). When enabled, a job's cost is computed + stored in **USD** via the versioned
    ``pricebook`` DB table and **displayed in INR** at ``fx_inr_per_usd``. The default pricebook
    version is the seeded ``placeholder-v0`` (every rate ``0.0`` → cost ``0``) until the owner
    inserts a real, calibrated version and points ``pricebook_version`` at it.

    ⚠ Even with ``enabled=True`` + a real pricebook, recorded cost stays **0** until a follow-up
    wires the worker to emit per-job ``usage`` (gpu/wall/egress/gemini) — M8 lands the ledger +
    pricebook + the recording seam; usage emission (the metering hooks) is a separate step."""

    enabled: bool = False
    pricebook_version: str = (
        "placeholder-v0"  # the seeded $0 version; owner flips to a real one
    )
    fx_inr_per_usd: float = 83.0  # USD→INR display rate (Bangalore market)
    margin: float = 0.0  # resale markup (≥0); 0 = bill at cost

28	# Async job executor (DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN #16). max_workers=1 on purpose: a single
29	# self-hosted box serializes GPU work, and the Gemini provider is rate-fragile under
30	# parallel uploads (2026-06-10 ops learning: serial uploads + backoff). The segmenters
31	# already parallelize their AI handoff internally via their own pools.
32	#
33	# ⚠ O2 RISK (CODE_CLEANUP_AUDIT §3c): a single worker means ONE stuck or hung job
34	# (e.g. hung Gemini generate, hung WSL GPU call, hung Cloud Run poll) blocks ALL
35	# subsequent jobs — they stay 'queued' forever. Mitigations already in place:
36	#   - ExecutionPolicy op_timeout_sec kills hung generate/processing calls
37	#   - detector timeout_sec kills hung WSL/GPU calls
38	#   - Cloud Run poll has a max_wait_sec bound
39	#   - ffmpeg/ffprobe subprocesses use bounded media timeouts
40	# A future slice should add a per-job wall-clock timeout (the executor itself
41	# cannot enforce timeouts on the Thread).

### 421. assistant (2026-07-04T04:08:53.844Z)

"""Drain-executor concurrency (#466).

The in-process job drain is serial (max_workers=1) for truly-local — a single box serializes GPU
work and the Gemini provider is rate-fragile. Under cloud_run the box only DISPATCHES + bucket-polls
(I/O-bound; GPU is on ephemeral L4s, WASB-only has no Gemini), so it scales to
``deployment.max_concurrent_remote_jobs``. The worker count is fixed at first executor creation from
the config; everything else stays byte-identical to the serial default.
"""

import pytest

import backend.api.job_worker as jw
from backend.config.models import AppConfig


def _reset_executor():
    if jw._job_executor is not None:
        jw._job_executor.shutdown(wait=False)
    jw._job_executor = None


def test_resolve_drain_workers_serial_by_default():
    assert jw._resolve_drain_workers(None) == 1  # no config (tests / non-server)
    assert jw._resolve_drain_workers(AppConfig()) == 1  # default compute_target="" → serial


def test_resolve_drain_workers_scales_only_under_cloud_run():
    cloud = AppConfig.model_validate(
        {"deployment": {"compute_target": "cloud_run", "max_concurrent_remote_jobs": 5}}
    )
    assert jw._resolve_drain_workers(cloud) == 5
    # a truly-local (local_gpu) target stays serial even with a high knob — the knob is cloud_run-only
    local = AppConfig.model_validate(
        {"deployment": {"compute_target": "local_gpu", "max_concurrent_remote_jobs": 5}}
    )
    assert jw._resolve_drain_workers(local) == 1


def test_get_executor_uses_configured_worker_count():
    _reset_executor()
    try:
        cfg = AppConfig.model_validate(
            {"deployment": {"compute_target": "cloud_run", "max_concurrent_remote_jobs": 6}}
        )
        assert jw._get_executor(cfg)._max_workers == 6
    finally:
        _reset_executor()


def test_get_executor_serial_without_config():
    _reset_executor()
    try:
        assert jw._get_executor()._max_workers == 1  # default → today's serial behaviour
    finally:
        _reset_executor()


def test_max_concurrent_remote_jobs_is_bounded():
    with pytest.raises(Exception):
        AppConfig.model_validate({"deployment": {"max_concurrent_remote_jobs": 0}})  # ge=1
    with pytest.raises(Exception):
        AppConfig.model_validate({"deployment": {"max_concurrent_remote_jobs": 999}})  # le=64

"""Drain-executor concurrency (#466).

The in-process job drain is serial (max_workers=1) for truly-local — a single box serializes GPU
work and the Gemini provider is rate-fragile. Under cloud_run the box only DISPATCHES + bucket-polls
(I/O-bound; GPU is on ephemeral L4s, WASB-only has no Gemini), so it scales to
``deployment.max_concurrent_remote_jobs``. The worker count is fixed at first executor creation from
the config (``configure_drain``); everything else stays byte-identical to the serial default.
"""

import pytest
from pydantic import ValidationError

import backend.api.job_worker as jw
from backend.config.models import AppConfig


def _reset():
    if jw._job_executor is not None:
        jw._job_executor.shutdown(wait=False)
    jw._job_executor = None
    jw._drain_max_workers = 1


def test_resolve_drain_workers_serial_by_default():
    assert jw._resolve_drain_workers(None) == 1  # no config (tests / non-server)
    assert jw._resolve_drain_workers(AppConfig()) == 1  # default compute_target="" → serial


def test_resolve_drain_workers_scales_only_under_cloud_run():
    cloud = AppConfig.model_validate(
        {"deployment": {"compute_target": "cloud_run", "max_concurrent_remote_jobs": 5}}
    )
    assert jw._resolve_drain_workers(cloud) == 5
    # a truly-local (local_gpu) target stays serial even with a high knob — the knob is cloud_run-only
    local = AppConfig.model_validate(
        {"deployment": {"compute_target": "local_gpu", "max_concurrent_remote_jobs": 5}}
    )
    assert jw._resolve_drain_workers(local) == 1


def test_configure_drain_sets_executor_worker_count():
    _reset()
    try:
        jw.configure_drain(
            AppConfig.model_validate(
                {
                    "deployment": {
                        "compute_target": "cloud_run",
                        "max_concurrent_remote_jobs": 6,
                    }
                }
            )
        )
        assert jw._get_executor()._max_workers == 6
    finally:
        _reset()


def test_executor_serial_without_configure():
    _reset()
    try:
        assert jw._get_executor()._max_workers == 1  # default → today's serial behaviour
    finally:
        _reset()


def test_max_concurrent_remote_jobs_is_bounded():
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        AppConfig.model_validate({"deployment": {"max_concurrent_remote_jobs": 0}})  # ge=1
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        AppConfig.model_validate({"deployment": {"max_concurrent_remote_jobs": 999}})  # le=64

def test_repo_config_json_has_no_profile():
    """The shipped config.json must not silently opt into a profile (default path stays today's
    behaviour for the real deployment)."""
    repo_root = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
    cfg_path = os.path.join(repo_root, "config.json")
    if not os.path.exists(cfg_path):
        pytest.skip("no repo config.json")
    cfg = load_config(cfg_path)
    assert cfg.deployment.profile == ""

=== /api/highlights endpoint ===
@app.get("/api/highlights/{video_id}/{filename}")
def download_highlight(video_id: str, filename: str, request: Request):
    """Stream a compiled highlight reel — `filename` is the bare name given to /api/compile
    (which only writes into output_dir; the browser previously got back an unreachable
    server-local path)."""
    try:
        name = safe_output_filename(filename)
    except ValueError as e:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=str(e)) from e
    if not request.app.state.db.get_video(video_id):
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="Indexed video record not found.")
    output_dir = (
        getattr(
            getattr(request.app.state.config, "output", None), "output_dir", "output"
        )
        or "output"
    )
    path = os.path.join(output_dir, name)
    try:
        path = resolve_under_root(path, output_dir)
    except ValueError as e:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=str(e)) from e
    if not os.path.isfile(path):
        raise HTTPException(
            status_code=404,
            detail=f"No compiled file named '{name}'. Compile first via /api/compile.",
        )
    media = "video/quicktime" if name.lower().endswith(".mov") else "video/mp4"
    return FileResponse(path, media_type=media, filename=name)

    notify("stitching")
    local_src = ""
    try:
        # The stored `filepath` is the SOURCE ref (gs://, s3://, gdrive://, or a local path). Resolve to
        # a LOCAL file the stitcher can read — identity for a local path, fetch-to-scratch for a URI (the
        # same seam the process path uses); without it, compiling a bucket-ingested video can't open gs://.
        local_src = storage.acquire_local_input(
            video["filepath"], getattr(cfg, "storage", None)
        )
        out_path = compiler.compile_highlights(local_src, time_pairs, out_name)
    except Exception as e:  # noqa: BLE001 — a stitch/ffmpeg failure is a clean job FAILURE, not a crash
        logger.error(
            "[compile] stitching failed for video_id=%s: %s", video_id, e, exc_info=True
        )
        return {
            "status": "failed",
            "message": f"Highlight stitching failed: {e}",
            "video_id": video_id,
        }
    finally:
        if local_src:
            storage.cleanup_acquired_local_input(
                video["filepath"], local_src, getattr(cfg, "storage", None)
            )

    # `filepath` is server-local (not browser-reachable); also return the download URL so the SPA needn't
    # hand-build it (P6 / PER_RALLY_SELECTION.md §4.3).
    download_url = f"/api/highlights/{quote(video_id)}/{quote(out_name)}"
    notify("finished")
    return {
        "status": "success",
        "filepath": out_path,
        "download_url": download_url,
        "video_id": video_id,
    }

### 518. assistant (2026-07-04T07:48:24.317Z)

Important — the CI caught a real design issue in my #465 fix. The failing test `test_process_hosted_mode_rejects_nonlocal_uri` asserts hosted mode **rejects** `gs://` URIs. That's a deliberate **tenant-isolation** control: in hosted mode, an authenticated user shouldn't be able to point the SA at *arbitrary* buckets. My blanket "skip the jail for all `scheme://`" was too permissive. Let me read the test to design a tighter fix (allow gs:// only under the configured `input_root`):

### 519. user (2026-07-04T07:48:26.550Z)

def test_process_hosted_mode_rejects_nonlocal_uri(env):
    """Hosted mode (allowed_video_root set) must reject a bucket/Drive URI — a tenant can't point
    the engine at an arbitrary bucket. Pins the security contract instead of an incidental side effect."""
    client, db, tmp_path, mp = env
    root = tmp_path / "tenant"
    root.mkdir()
    mp.setattr(m.global_config.security, "allowed_video_root", str(root))
    for bad in ("gs://b/x.mp4", "s3://b/x.mp4", "gdrive://Sharing/x.mp4"):
        r = client.post("/api/process", json={"video_path": bad})
        assert r.status_code == 400, f"{bad} -> {r.status_code}"
    # A local file UNDER the root is still accepted (passes validation; processing mocked elsewhere).
    under = root / "ok.mp4"
    under.write_bytes(b"hashable")
    mp.setattr(m.registry, "run_all_with_context", lambda *a, **k: (_pass(), {}))
    mp.setattr(m.segmenter_registry, "get", lambda name: _FakeSeg)
    r = client.post("/api/process", json={"video_path": str(under), "segmenter": "x"})
    assert r.status_code == 202


def test_cli_nonlocal_input_fetches_and_keeps_uri(tmp_path, monkeypatch):

### 520. assistant (2026-07-04T07:49:21.870Z)

The test confirms it — hosted mode deliberately rejects arbitrary bucket URIs (tenant isolation), and the test uses no `input_root`. The **correct** fix: allow a bucket URI only if it's under the configured `storage.input_root` (my M9 flow's `gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/videos/...` qualifies; an arbitrary `gs://b/x` does not). My blanket-allow was wrong. Let me revise PR #465. First locate its branch/worktree:

### 521. user (2026-07-04T07:49:25.546Z)

=== branch exists? ===
  fix/allowed-video-root-uri

fatal: not a git repository (or any of the parent directories): .git
=== current validate_input_path in that branch ===

# A storage URI (gs://, s3://, gdrive://, …) starts with a scheme and is NOT a local filesystem
# path, so the allowed_root containment jail must not be applied to it: os.path.realpath("gs://b/x")
# mangles the URI into a bogus cwd-relative path and would spuriously reject a legitimate bucket
# input. Bucket access is governed by the storage config + cloud IAM, not this local-path jail.
_URI_SCHEME_RE = re.compile(r"^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.\-]*://")


def validate_input_path(path: str, allowed_root: Optional[str] = None) -> None:
    """Reject null bytes and (when an allowed_root is configured) out-of-root LOCAL inputs.

    With no allowed_root (default, single-user local mode) only the null-byte check applies, so the
    local 'point it at any of my videos' workflow is preserved. Setting an allowed_root (a hosted/
    tenant deployment) enforces containment for LOCAL paths. A storage URI (gs://, s3://, gdrive://)
    is not a local filesystem path, so the containment jail does NOT apply to it — realpath() would
    mangle it and spuriously reject a legitimate bucket input; its access is scoped by the storage
    config + IAM instead. The null-byte check always applies.
    """
    if "\x00" in path:
        raise ValueError("video_path must not contain a null byte")
    if allowed_root and not _URI_SCHEME_RE.match(path):
        resolve_under_root(path, allowed_root)

$repo = "Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer"
$prs = @(462, 465, 467, 468, 469)
$baseline = [int]($env:BASELINE)  # total review+comment count across OPEN PRs already handled
$log = "C:\Users\avidu\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-avidu-Projects-badminton-highlight-indexer\90c0b6b8-7430-4870-98cb-e487eacfa1d7\scratchpad\reviews_watch.log"
$res = "C:\Users\avidu\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-avidu-Projects-badminton-highlight-indexer\90c0b6b8-7430-4870-98cb-e487eacfa1d7\scratchpad\reviews_state.txt"
Add-Content -Path $log -Value "watch start (baseline=$baseline) prs=$($prs -join ',')"
for ($i = 0; $i -lt 220; $i++) {
    $total = 0; $merged = 0; $lines = @()
    foreach ($pr in $prs) {
        $j = gh pr view $pr --repo $repo --json number,state,reviewDecision,reviews,comments 2>$null | ConvertFrom-Json
        if (-not $j) { $lines += "PR#$pr ERR"; continue }
        if ($j.state -eq "MERGED") { $merged++; $lines += "PR#$pr MERGED"; continue }
        $rc = ($j.reviews.Count + $j.comments.Count)
        $total += $rc
        $lines += "PR#$pr $($j.state) decision=$($j.reviewDecision) reviews=$($j.reviews.Count) comments=$($j.comments.Count)"
    }
    $ts = Get-Date -Format "HH:mm:ss"
    if ($merged -eq $prs.Count) {
        Set-Content -Path $res -Value ("ALL_MERGED`n" + ($lines -join "`n"))
        Add-Content -Path $log -Value "[$ts] ALL MERGED"
        break
    }
    if ($total -gt $baseline) {
        Set-Content -Path $res -Value ("ACTIVITY total=$total baseline=$baseline`n" + ($lines -join "`n"))
        Add-Content -Path $log -Value "[$ts] ACTIVITY total=$total > baseline=$baseline"
        break
    }
    Add-Content -Path $log -Value "[$ts] iter=$i quiet total=$total merged=$merged/5"
    Start-Sleep -Seconds 300
}

### 550. user (2026-07-04T07:56:52.204Z)

======== PR #465 comments ========
[avidullu] Blocking: the full offline suite is currently red on this PR, and the failure is tied to the behavior change here rather than CI infra.

`tests/test_api_endpoints.py::test_process_hosted_mode_rejects_nonlocal_uri` still asserts that `gs://`, `s3://`, and `gdrive://` inputs return 400 when `allowed_video_root` is set. This PR changes `validate_input_path()` so storage URIs skip the local filesystem jail and now return 202, so the suite fails with:

`AssertionError: gs://b/x.mp4 -> 202; assert 202 == 400`

Please reconcile that existing hosted-mode contract test with the new security boundary before merge. If storage URIs are intentionally allowed under hosted edge-auth, update/remove the old counterweight test and nearby comments that still say hosted mode rejects non-local URIs. If only trusted/configured buckets should be allowed, add that allowlist check explicitly and cover it here. Either way, the full suite needs to go green before this can land.

--- inline review comments (code) ---

======== PR #467 comments ========
[avidullu] Potential blocker for non-default storage configs: the new staging upload drops the active storage settings.

In `backend/orchestration.py`, the local-upload path builds `staged_uri` from `storage.input_root` but calls:

`storage.put(local_src, staged_uri)`

without passing the current storage config. Other storage operations in the repo pass the model dump into `storage.fetch/put/list_uris` so backend-specific settings survive. Without that here, staging ignores configured settings such as `storage.s3.endpoint_url` / region / addressing style for R2, DO Spaces, or MinIO, and also any GCS project/client/signed settings. A cloud-serving config with `input_root="s3://..."` plus an endpoint URL would try to construct a default S3 client instead of the configured one; even for GCS it can target the wrong/default ADC context.

Please thread the same `storage_cfg.model_dump()` settings into this `storage.put(..., config=storage_dict)` call, and add a small regression test with a non-default backend setting or injected fake to prove the config reaches the backend.

--- inline review comments (code) ---

======== PR #469 comments ========
[avidullu] Potential blocker for configured object stores: the new mirrored-reel path does not pass the active storage config to the storage layer.

Two new call sites use only the URI:

- `backend/orchestration.py`: `storage.put(out_path, reel_uri)`
- `backend/main.py`: `storage.exists(reel_uri)` and `storage.signed_url(reel_uri, method="GET")`

That bypasses backend-specific settings from `cfg.storage`, unlike the existing fetch/put/list paths that pass a storage settings dict. For S3-compatible stores this drops `endpoint_url`, region, and addressing style, so R2/DO Spaces/MinIO signed URLs can be generated against the wrong/default client. For GCS it also drops configured project/client fields and `signed_url_ttl`, which is especially relevant for this PR's signing path.

Please derive the storage settings from the active config and pass them into all three calls (`put`, `exists`, and `signed_url`). A regression test with an injected/fake backend config would catch this: the mirror should upload with the config, and `/api/highlights` should check/sign with the same config rather than constructing a fresh default backend.

--- inline review comments (code) ---

### 552. user (2026-07-04T07:57:39.394Z)

=== get_storage: how config maps to backends ===
def get_storage(
    backend: Optional[str] = None,
    *,
    config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
    **overrides,
) -> StorageBackend:
    """Construct a backend. ``backend`` (or ``config['backend']``, default 'local') selects
    the implementation; its per-backend settings come from ``config[backend]`` merged with
    ``overrides``. Settings carry NO secrets — only endpoints/regions/paths/source-selectors."""
    cfg = config or {}
    name = backend or cfg.get("backend", "local")
    # Per-backend settings live under the backend name; tolerate the hyphen→underscore form so a
    # hyphenated backend ("gdrive-api") can read a pydantic field that can't contain a hyphen
    # ("gdrive_api"). Hyphen-free names are unaffected (the first lookup wins).
    settings: Dict[str, Any] = dict(
        cfg.get(name) or cfg.get(name.replace("-", "_")) or {}
    )
    settings.update(overrides)
    return _backend_class(name)(**settings)

--- reel_object_uri body ---
def reel_object_uri(cfg, video_id: str, name: str) -> str:
    """The bucket URI a compiled reel is mirrored to for a direct signed-URL download (#463), or "" when
    the output isn't a cloud bucket (truly-local → serve the local file). Shared by the compile
    mirror-upload and the /api/highlights redirect: ``<output_root>/highlights/<video_id>/<name>``."""
    root = (getattr(getattr(cfg, "storage", None), "output_root", "") or "").strip()
    if not root or "://" not in root:
        return ""
    return f"{root.rstrip('/')}/highlights/{video_id}/{name}"


def _execute_compile(cfg, db, job: Dict[str, Any], progress=None) -> Dict[str, Any]:

### 606. user (2026-07-04T08:13:39.887Z)

======== PR #462 ========
reviewDecision=
[avidullu] LGTM for handoff.

Verified locally on this PR head:
- `python -m ruff check .`
- `python -m mypy backend training`
- `python run_all_tests.py --cov=backend --cov=training --cov-report=term --cov-fail-under=70`

All passed, coverage floor met, and I have no remaining review findings on this PR.

======== PR #468 ========
reviewDecision=
[avidullu] Withdrawing my earlier LGTM pending a logic clarification/fix.

On re-review, this changes the *global* job-drain executor size based only on `deployment.compute_target == "cloud_run"`, but the jobs flowing through that executor are not all remote Cloud Run detect jobs:

- On a `cloud_run` server, compile jobs (`kind='compile'`) and explicit `compute="local"` process jobs will now also run concurrently, even though the stated rationale for raising the worker count is that remote Cloud Run dispatch/poll is I/O-bound and does not consume the local GPU/Gemini path. That weakens the existing serialization invariant for CPU-heavy ffmpeg compile and local override work.
- Conversely, the existing per-request cloud override path allows a local-default server to dispatch Cloud Run when `_cloud_available(cfg)` is true (`compute="cloud"` + env/output bucket), but `_resolve_drain_workers()` still returns 1 because `compute_target` is not `cloud_run`. Those cloud jobs remain serialized despite being the same I/O-bound dispatch/poll shape.

So the current knob is really "make every async job on a cloud_run-default server concurrent", not "allow N concurrent remote Cloud Run jobs". If that broader behavior is intentional, the PR should say so and add tests covering compile/local-override concurrency. If the intended contract is remote-detect-only concurrency, the drain needs to classify jobs by effective compute path (or use separate executors/limits) so remote dispatch can parallelize without accidentally parallelizing local/compile work, and so `compute="cloud"` overrides get the same treatment.

=== _resolve_drain_workers ===
def _resolve_drain_workers(config: Any = None) -> int:
    """Drain concurrency (#466): cloud_run dispatch is I/O-bound (dispatch a Cloud Run Job + bucket-poll
    its done-marker — the GPU work is on ephemeral L4s, WASB-only has no Gemini), so it may run
    ``deployment.max_concurrent_remote_jobs`` in PARALLEL. Every other target stays serial (1): a
    truly-local box serializes GPU work and the Gemini provider is rate-fragile (the rationale above).
    Defaults to 1 when config is absent (tests / non-server) → behaviour unchanged."""
    dep = getattr(config, "deployment", None)
    if getattr(dep, "compute_target", "") == "cloud_run":
        return max(1, int(getattr(dep, "max_concurrent_remote_jobs", 1) or 1))
    return 1

## (B) FULL E2E DEPLOY TEST — run AFTER all four PRs are merged

Goal: prove the merged fixes work on a real deployed build — the >32 MiB signed-URL download (#463) and
the browser-upload→gs:// staging (#461) — then restore the live edge-auth state and flip A1 → DONE.

1. **Rebuild the worker image from merged master** (has #461/#463/#465/#468 backend code + #462's
   `.[storage-gcs,cloud-run]`): `gcloud builds submit --config=deploy/cloudrun/cloudbuild.yaml` (heavy
   CUDA build, ~15 min; region asia-southeast1). ⚠ The base Dockerfile installs `.[storage-gcs,cloud-run]`
   but NOT the `auth` extra (PyJWT[crypto]) that M9 edge-auth needs — the *deployed* image gets PyJWT via
   a derived overlay. **Recommended follow-up:** add `auth` to the base Dockerfile so a rebuild is
   M9-ready (a small 6th PR). For the e2e itself, edge-auth is OFF, so PyJWT isn't required.
2. **Build the derived control-plane image** = base + PyJWT: context `Dockerfile` = `FROM
   asia-southeast1-docker.pkg.dev/gen-lang-client-0306026826/rally/rally-worker:latest` +
   `RUN python3 -m pip install --no-cache-dir "google-cloud-run>=0.10,<1" "PyJWT[crypto]>=2.8"`; tag
   `.../rally/rally-control-plane:latest`. `gcloud builds submit <ctx> --tag <img> --region asia-southeast1`.
   (No more paths.py/backend overlays needed once the fixes are IN the base image — the prior session
   overlaid #465's OLD blanket paths.py into the live image; the rebuild replaces it with the corrected code.)
3. **Deploy CP with edge-auth OFF** so you can drive it with an identity token (no CF login needed for
   the test). Regenerate the service YAML (config below) with `EDGE_AUTH` unset and
   `gcloud run services replace <yaml> --region asia-southeast1`. Run gcloud from **PowerShell**.
4. **Run the CUJ + verify** (private service → auth every curl with
   `-H "Authorization: Bearer $(gcloud auth print-identity-token)"`; URL below):
   - `POST /api/process {"video_path":"gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/videos/MahadevpuraSingles.MP4","segmenter":"wasb_hybrid","compute":"cloud"}`
     → poll `/api/jobs/{id}` to `done` (L4 run ~13 min) → note segment ids.
   - `POST /api/compile {"video_id":<id>,"segment_ids":[1..N],"output_filename":"e2e.mp4"}` → poll to done.
   - **#463 check:** `GET /api/highlights/{video_id}/e2e.mp4` should now **307-redirect** to a
     `storage.googleapis.com` signed URL, and `curl -L` should download the FULL >32 MiB reel (no edge-500).
     Also confirm the reel is mirrored at `gs://khelsutra-rally-serving/highlights/<video_id>/e2e.mp4`.
   - **#461 check:** upload a small mp4 via `/api/upload/init`→`/part/0`→`/complete`, then `POST /api/process`
     with the returned LOCAL path + `compute:cloud` → it should STAGE to gs:// and dispatch (not hard-fail
     with "cloud-serving needs a cloud input"). (Signing was already verified: `gcloud storage sign-url`
     impersonating the compute SA produced a working URL — the SA has self-`serviceAccountTokenCreator`.)

   live posture is back. Verify `/api/process` sans CF JWT → 401 and `https://api.khelsutra.guru/api/health`

## Gotchas (learned the hard way)
- Run **gcloud from PowerShell**, not git-bash (MSYS mangles `/gcs`, `/tmp`); quote comma-dict flags
  (`--add-volume "name=…,type=…"`) or the cmd-shim splits them.
- The CP uses per-instance **ephemeral SQLite + /tmp** — DB/reel don't survive a redeploy or a second
  instance. That's WHY #463 mirrors reels to gs:// + #461 restages uploads. maxScale is 3.
- **Pre-merge:** run the WHOLE suite locally, not per-file (the #465 CI-red lesson).

## Key decisions (don't relitigate)
- WASB-only alpha (`skip_ai_handoff=true`); config.json wins over the cloud-serving preset (loader.py:194).
- Reuse the worker image as the CP (+ google-cloud-run + PyJWT); IAM-private + CF Access is the tester auth.
- #468 concurrency: serialize local/compile via `_LOCAL_COMPUTE_LANE`, parallelize only remote polls; the
  *park*-instead-of-*block* refinement is offered to the reviewer (do it only if they ask).
- A1 stays NOT-DONE until the 4 PRs merge + the e2e passes; then flip it.

### 667. assistant (2026-07-04T08:33:30.883Z)

The watcher caught a **new comment on #468** — avidullu found "one more blocker before I can re-LGTM" about `claim_due_job()` under parallelism. Let me read it in full:

### 668. user (2026-07-04T08:33:33.403Z)

I found one more blocker before I can re-LGTM.

`claim_due_job()` is CAS-safe for avoiding double-claims, but under parallel drain workers the losers return `None` (`backend/infrastructure/database_jobs.py:357-380`). `_drain_due_jobs()` then treats `None` as "queue empty" and exits (`backend/api/job_worker.py:273-276`). With `max_workers > 1`, multiple workers can select the same first queued row; one updates it, and the losers stop even though later jobs are still queued.

I reproduced locally on this head with 8 queued jobs and 8 concurrent claimers:

```text
[None, None, None, None, 'j0', None, None, 'j1'] -> only 2/8 claims
```

For long Cloud Run bucket-poll jobs this can leave dispatch capacity unused and queued jobs waiting until another submit/wake or a running worker finally frees up, which undermines the concurrency improvement this PR is trying to provide.

Suggested fix: make `claim_due_job()` retry on `rowcount != 1` until it either successfully claims a row or a fresh SELECT finds no runnable rows, or return a distinct lost-race sentinel so `_drain_due_jobs()` loops instead of treating it as empty. Please add a regression test with concurrent claimers/drains over N queued jobs proving there is no false-empty result / that N distinct jobs can be claimed.

### 669. assistant (2026-07-04T08:34:11.330Z)

The reviewer found a **genuine, reproduced concurrency bug** — and it's exactly the kind #468 introduces: `claim_due_job()` returns `None` when a worker *loses* the CAS race, and `_drain_due_jobs()` treats that `None` as "queue empty" and exits. So with parallel workers, the losers give up prematurely (his repro: only 2/8 jobs claimed). This is a real blocker for #468.
